What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what made me come in is that I’ve started losing my place at work. I'm a receptionist, and lately, while I’m scheduling appointments or working on the lobby displays, my mind just... slips off track. It's like I can't focus on what's right in front of me because I’m busy thinking about what I might have overlooked.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen that you think might have started these problems with focusing at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. This has been going on for about nine months now. It basically started ever since... well, there was this overnight detention, and a big mix-up with the paperwork around it. That's pretty much when all these issues began.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the mix-up with the paperwork or the detention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's like I can't stop it. I can tell myself, 'Sunny, it's handled, it's in the past,' but the thought just keeps looping. And then, pretty soon, I've gone from worrying about that paperwork to another concern, and then another, until I'm just checking all these little details in my head over and over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of these looping thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed myself avoiding anything. I still go to work and do my normal routine, run my errands... it's just that my mind is running through all those worries while I'm doing it. But I wouldn't say I stay away from any specific situations or things because of the thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when these worries or looping thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches when I'm worrying. I mean, I don't feel like I have to go wash my hands a bunch of times or keep checking if the stove is off. It's more just... checking things in my head, if that makes sense. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.0, F43.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, what made me come in is that I’ve started losing my place at work. I'm a receptionist, and lately, while I’m scheduling appointments or working on the lobby displays, my mind just... slips off track. It's like I can't focus on what's right in front of me because I’m busy thinking about what I might have overlooked.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific happen that you think might have started these problems with focusing at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. This has been going on for about nine months now. It basically started ever since... well, there was this overnight detention, and a big mix-up with the paperwork around it. That's pretty much when all these issues began.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the mix-up with the paperwork or the detention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's like I can't stop it. I can tell myself, 'Sunny, it's handled, it's in the past,' but the thought just keeps looping. And then, pretty soon, I've gone from worrying about that paperwork to another concern, and then another, until I'm just checking all these little details in my head over and over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of these looping thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed myself avoiding anything. I still go to work and do my normal routine, run my errands... it's just that my mind is running through all those worries while I'm doing it. But I wouldn't say I stay away from any specific situations or things because of the thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when these worries or looping thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches when I'm worrying. It's really just the thoughts themselves that are the problem, not so much physical pain or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to do certain things over and over, like checking or cleaning, because of these worries?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I have to go wash my hands a bunch of times or keep checking if the stove is off. It's more just... checking things in my head, if that makes sense. But physically doing things over and over? No, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind that you try to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely repeat. I try to stop them. I'll tell myself, Sunny, it's handled, but the thought just keeps looping anyway. And pretty soon I've gone from that one concern right to another one, and I just end up checking all these details in my head over and over again. So I do try to ignore them or push them away, but they just keep running.\n\nDoctor: Are these looping thoughts you're experiencing unwanted or intrusive?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word intrusive. I mean, obviously I don't want to be worrying all the time, so I guess they're unwanted in that way. But it's not like weird or random thoughts are just popping into my head out of nowhere. They're just my normal, everyday concerns about work or my schedule... they just kind of get stuck on a loop and I can't seem to turn them off.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these looping thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I mean, it's definitely annoying. It's frustrating when I just want to relax and my brain won't shut off. But it's not like I'm falling apart over it or anything like that. It just kind of wears me out, having to deal with it all the time... but I still get through my day fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they're unreasonable. Like I said, they're just normal things I need to take care of—like scheduling at the clinic or making sure bills are paid. It's just the amount of time I spend thinking about them that feels excessive, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: How much do these looping thoughts and worries affect your ability to perform your daily tasks and enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m still showing up and getting through the day just fine. But... I guess it does slow me down a bit at work. Like, scheduling takes me a lot longer now because I end up double-checking myself so much. And there have been a few times recently where I’ve had to redo parts of the lobby displays because I just... lost track of what I was arranging. So I'm still getting my tasks done, it just takes more effort than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still do all the same things I used to. I still like my hobbies and hanging out with my family. The worries don't really stop me from doing anything, they're just... there in the background while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries or looping thoughts affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my family might notice that I'm a little distracted sometimes if my mind is on other things, but it hasn't caused any arguments or real problems. We still get along fine, and I still see my friends like usual. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships. It's mostly just something I'm dealing with internally.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily about the original mix-up with the paperwork or have they expanded to include a wide range of everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely expanded. I mean, it started with that original mix-up, but now... I just find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Like work schedules, bills, travel plans... even just little paperwork errors. It's like my brain tells me that one small mix-up is going to somehow turn into a much bigger problem. So, yeah, it's definitely not just that first thing anymore, it's a lot of everyday stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. No, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind that you try to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely repeat. I try to stop them. I'll tell myself, Sunny, it's handled, but the thought just keeps looping anyway. And pretty soon I've gone from that one concern right to another one, and I just end up checking all these details in my head over and over again. So I do try to ignore them or push them away, but they just keep running.\n\nDoctor: Are these looping thoughts you're experiencing unwanted or intrusive?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word intrusive. I mean, obviously I don't want to be worrying all the time, so I guess they're unwanted in that way. But it's not like weird or random thoughts are just popping into my head out of nowhere. They're just my normal, everyday concerns about work or my schedule... they just kind of get stuck on a loop and I can't seem to turn them off.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these looping thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I mean, it's definitely annoying. It's frustrating when I just want to relax and my brain won't shut off. But it's not like I'm falling apart over it or anything like that. It just kind of wears me out, having to deal with it all the time... but I still get through my day fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they're unreasonable. Like I said, they're just normal things I need to take care of—like scheduling at the clinic or making sure bills are paid. It's just the amount of time I spend thinking about them that feels excessive, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: How much do these looping thoughts and worries affect your ability to perform your daily tasks and enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m still showing up and getting through the day just fine. But... I guess it does slow me down a bit at work. Like, scheduling takes me a lot longer now because I end up double-checking myself so much. And there have been a few times recently where I’ve had to redo parts of the lobby displays because I just... lost track of what I was arranging. So I'm still getting my tasks done, it just takes more effort than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still do all the same things I used to. I still like my hobbies and hanging out with my family. The worries don't really stop me from doing anything, they're just... there in the background while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries or looping thoughts affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my family might notice that I'm a little distracted sometimes if my mind is on other things, but it hasn't caused any arguments or real problems. We still get along fine, and I still see my friends like usual. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships. It's mostly just something I'm dealing with internally.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily about the original mix-up with the paperwork or have they expanded to include a wide range of everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely expanded. I mean, it started with that original mix-up, but now... I just find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Like work schedules, bills, travel plans... even just little paperwork errors. It's like my brain tells me that one small mix-up is going to somehow turn into a much bigger problem. So, yeah, it's definitely not just that first thing anymore, it's a lot of everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts would stop or significantly decrease if the original cause, such as the mix-up with the paperwork, was fully resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I mean... it would definitely be a relief to have that original thing completely sorted out, but since my mind has already started stressing over bills and schedules and all those other things... I don't know if fixing that first problem would actually make the rest of it stop now. I'd like to think it would help, but honestly, I just don't know.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. And then, pretty soon, I've gone from worrying about that paperwork to another concern, and then another, until I'm just checking all these little details in my head over and over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of these looping thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed myself avoiding anything. I still go to work and do my normal routine, run my errands... it's just that my mind is running through all those worries while I'm doing it. But I wouldn't say I stay away from any specific situations or things because of the thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when these worries or looping thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches when I'm worrying. It's really just the thoughts themselves that are the problem, not so much physical pain or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to do certain things over and over, like checking or cleaning, because of these worries?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I have to go wash my hands a bunch of times or keep checking if the stove is off. It's more just... checking things in my head, if that makes sense. But physically doing things over and over? No, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind that you try to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely repeat. I try to stop them. I'll tell myself, Sunny, it's handled, but the thought just keeps looping anyway. And pretty soon I've gone from that one concern right to another one, and I just end up checking all these details in my head over and over again. So I do try to ignore them or push them away, but they just keep running.\n\nDoctor: Are these looping thoughts you're experiencing unwanted or intrusive?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word intrusive. I mean, obviously I don't want to be worrying all the time, so I guess they're unwanted in that way. But it's not like weird or random thoughts are just popping into my head out of nowhere. They're just my normal, everyday concerns about work or my schedule... they just kind of get stuck on a loop and I can't seem to turn them off.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these looping thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I mean, it's definitely annoying. It's frustrating when I just want to relax and my brain won't shut off. But it's not like I'm falling apart over it or anything like that. It just kind of wears me out, having to deal with it all the time... but I still get through my day fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they're unreasonable. Like I said, they're just normal things I need to take care of—like scheduling at the clinic or making sure bills are paid. It's just the amount of time I spend thinking about them that feels excessive, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: How much do these looping thoughts and worries affect your ability to perform your daily tasks and enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m still showing up and getting through the day just fine. But... I guess it does slow me down a bit at work. Like, scheduling takes me a lot longer now because I end up double-checking myself so much. And there have been a few times recently where I’ve had to redo parts of the lobby displays because I just... lost track of what I was arranging. So I'm still getting my tasks done, it just takes more effort than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still do all the same things I used to. I still like my hobbies and hanging out with my family. The worries don't really stop me from doing anything, they're just... there in the background while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries or looping thoughts affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my family might notice that I'm a little distracted sometimes if my mind is on other things, but it hasn't caused any arguments or real problems. We still get along fine, and I still see my friends like usual. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships. It's mostly just something I'm dealing with internally.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily about the original mix-up with the paperwork or have they expanded to include a wide range of everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely expanded. I mean, it started with that original mix-up, but now... I just find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Like work schedules, bills, travel plans... even just little paperwork errors. It's like my brain tells me that one small mix-up is going to somehow turn into a much bigger problem. So, yeah, it's definitely not just that first thing anymore, it's a lot of everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts would stop or significantly decrease if the original cause, such as the mix-up with the paperwork, was fully resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I mean... it would definitely be a relief to have that original thing completely sorted out, but since my mind has already started stressing over bills and schedules and all those other things... I don't know if fixing that first problem would actually make the rest of it stop now. I'd like to think it would help, but honestly, I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily driven by a fear of something going wrong in general, or are they more about the consequences of specific events or stressors?\n\nPatient: I guess... I'm not really sure how to separate those two things. I mean, I worry about specific things like the bills or my schedule at work, but the feeling behind it is just this... general sense that something is going to get messed up. It's like I'm waiting for the other shoe to drop, so I don't know if it's the specific event or just the fear of things going wrong overall.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your looping thoughts and worries would be significantly reduced if you didn't have any upcoming events or tasks that you feel responsible for?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean... it sounds nice to not have anything on my plate for a while. But knowing how my mind works lately, I'd probably just find something else to stress over. Even if I didn't have any immediate tasks or responsibilities, I think I'd still be looping over things that could happen down the road. So... I don't really know if it would make a significant difference.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

No, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind that you try to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely repeat. I try to stop them. I'll tell myself, Sunny, it's handled, but the thought just keeps looping anyway. And pretty soon I've gone from that one concern right to another one, and I just end up checking all these details in my head over and over again. So I do try to ignore them or push them away, but they just keep running.\n\nDoctor: Are these looping thoughts you're experiencing unwanted or intrusive?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word intrusive. I mean, obviously I don't want to be worrying all the time, so I guess they're unwanted in that way. But it's not like weird or random thoughts are just popping into my head out of nowhere. They're just my normal, everyday concerns about work or my schedule... they just kind of get stuck on a loop and I can't seem to turn them off.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these looping thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I mean, it's definitely annoying. It's frustrating when I just want to relax and my brain won't shut off. But it's not like I'm falling apart over it or anything like that. It just kind of wears me out, having to deal with it all the time... but I still get through my day fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they're unreasonable. Like I said, they're just normal things I need to take care of—like scheduling at the clinic or making sure bills are paid. It's just the amount of time I spend thinking about them that feels excessive, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: How much do these looping thoughts and worries affect your ability to perform your daily tasks and enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m still showing up and getting through the day just fine. But... I guess it does slow me down a bit at work. Like, scheduling takes me a lot longer now because I end up double-checking myself so much. And there have been a few times recently where I’ve had to redo parts of the lobby displays because I just... lost track of what I was arranging. So I'm still getting my tasks done, it just takes more effort than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still do all the same things I used to. I still like my hobbies and hanging out with my family. The worries don't really stop me from doing anything, they're just... there in the background while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries or looping thoughts affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my family might notice that I'm a little distracted sometimes if my mind is on other things, but it hasn't caused any arguments or real problems. We still get along fine, and I still see my friends like usual. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships. It's mostly just something I'm dealing with internally.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily about the original mix-up with the paperwork or have they expanded to include a wide range of everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely expanded. I mean, it started with that original mix-up, but now... I just find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Like work schedules, bills, travel plans... even just little paperwork errors. It's like my brain tells me that one small mix-up is going to somehow turn into a much bigger problem. So, yeah, it's definitely not just that first thing anymore, it's a lot of everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts would stop or significantly decrease if the original cause, such as the mix-up with the paperwork, was fully resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I mean... it would definitely be a relief to have that original thing completely sorted out, but since my mind has already started stressing over bills and schedules and all those other things... I don't know if fixing that first problem would actually make the rest of it stop now. I'd like to think it would help, but honestly, I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily driven by a fear of something going wrong in general, or are they more about the consequences of specific events or stressors?\n\nPatient: I guess... I'm not really sure how to separate those two things. I mean, I worry about specific things like the bills or my schedule at work, but the feeling behind it is just this... general sense that something is going to get messed up. It's like I'm waiting for the other shoe to drop, so I don't know if it's the specific event or just the fear of things going wrong overall.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your looping thoughts and worries would be significantly reduced if you didn't have any upcoming events or tasks that you feel responsible for?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean... it sounds nice to not have anything on my plate for a while. But knowing how my mind works lately, I'd probably just find something else to stress over. Even if I didn't have any immediate tasks or responsibilities, I think I'd still be looping over things that could happen down the road. So... I don't really know if it would make a significant difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are more about everyday concerns in general or specifically about coping with the aftermath of the detention and paperwork mix-up?\n\nPatient: Well, I think it's definitely shifted to just everyday concerns now. I mean, the paperwork mix-up was the start, but lately... I find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Work schedules, paying the bills, travel plans, even just little, normal paperwork errors at my job. \n\nIt's just... it feels like if there's one small mix-up with any of it, it's going to snowball and turn into a much bigger problem. So yeah, it's definitely spread out to just regular, everyday stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

So I do try to ignore them or push them away, but they just keep running.\n\nDoctor: Are these looping thoughts you're experiencing unwanted or intrusive?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word intrusive. I mean, obviously I don't want to be worrying all the time, so I guess they're unwanted in that way. But it's not like weird or random thoughts are just popping into my head out of nowhere. They're just my normal, everyday concerns about work or my schedule... they just kind of get stuck on a loop and I can't seem to turn them off.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these looping thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I mean, it's definitely annoying. It's frustrating when I just want to relax and my brain won't shut off. But it's not like I'm falling apart over it or anything like that. It just kind of wears me out, having to deal with it all the time... but I still get through my day fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they're unreasonable. Like I said, they're just normal things I need to take care of—like scheduling at the clinic or making sure bills are paid. It's just the amount of time I spend thinking about them that feels excessive, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: How much do these looping thoughts and worries affect your ability to perform your daily tasks and enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m still showing up and getting through the day just fine. But... I guess it does slow me down a bit at work. Like, scheduling takes me a lot longer now because I end up double-checking myself so much. And there have been a few times recently where I’ve had to redo parts of the lobby displays because I just... lost track of what I was arranging. So I'm still getting my tasks done, it just takes more effort than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still do all the same things I used to. I still like my hobbies and hanging out with my family. The worries don't really stop me from doing anything, they're just... there in the background while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries or looping thoughts affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my family might notice that I'm a little distracted sometimes if my mind is on other things, but it hasn't caused any arguments or real problems. We still get along fine, and I still see my friends like usual. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships. It's mostly just something I'm dealing with internally.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily about the original mix-up with the paperwork or have they expanded to include a wide range of everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely expanded. I mean, it started with that original mix-up, but now... I just find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Like work schedules, bills, travel plans... even just little paperwork errors. It's like my brain tells me that one small mix-up is going to somehow turn into a much bigger problem. So, yeah, it's definitely not just that first thing anymore, it's a lot of everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts would stop or significantly decrease if the original cause, such as the mix-up with the paperwork, was fully resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I mean... it would definitely be a relief to have that original thing completely sorted out, but since my mind has already started stressing over bills and schedules and all those other things... I don't know if fixing that first problem would actually make the rest of it stop now. I'd like to think it would help, but honestly, I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily driven by a fear of something going wrong in general, or are they more about the consequences of specific events or stressors?\n\nPatient: I guess... I'm not really sure how to separate those two things. I mean, I worry about specific things like the bills or my schedule at work, but the feeling behind it is just this... general sense that something is going to get messed up. It's like I'm waiting for the other shoe to drop, so I don't know if it's the specific event or just the fear of things going wrong overall.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your looping thoughts and worries would be significantly reduced if you didn't have any upcoming events or tasks that you feel responsible for?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean... it sounds nice to not have anything on my plate for a while. But knowing how my mind works lately, I'd probably just find something else to stress over. Even if I didn't have any immediate tasks or responsibilities, I think I'd still be looping over things that could happen down the road. So... I don't really know if it would make a significant difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are more about everyday concerns in general or specifically about coping with the aftermath of the detention and paperwork mix-up?\n\nPatient: Well, I think it's definitely shifted to just everyday concerns now. I mean, the paperwork mix-up was the start, but lately... I find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Work schedules, paying the bills, travel plans, even just little, normal paperwork errors at my job. \n\nIt's just... it feels like if there's one small mix-up with any of it, it's going to snowball and turn into a much bigger problem. So yeah, it's definitely spread out to just regular, everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your looping thoughts and worries would completely go away if there were no stressors or problems in your life?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... it's kind of hard to even picture a life with absolutely zero problems. But if I had to guess... probably not. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, my mind just sort of looks for things to stress over right now. Even if everything was perfectly fine and there were no actual stressors, I feel like I'd still be sitting there waiting for something bad to happen. So, no, I don't think the worrying would just completely disappear.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

This has been going on for about nine months now. It basically started ever since... well, there was this overnight detention, and a big mix-up with the paperwork around it. That's pretty much when all these issues began.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the mix-up with the paperwork or the detention?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It's like I can't stop it. I can tell myself, 'Sunny, it's handled, it's in the past,' but the thought just keeps looping. And then, pretty soon, I've gone from worrying about that paperwork to another concern, and then another, until I'm just checking all these little details in my head over and over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding certain things or situations because of these looping thoughts or worries?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed myself avoiding anything. I still go to work and do my normal routine, run my errands... it's just that my mind is running through all those worries while I'm doing it. But I wouldn't say I stay away from any specific situations or things because of the thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any physical symptoms like headaches or stomachaches when these worries or looping thoughts occur?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't get headaches or stomachaches when I'm worrying. It's really just the thoughts themselves that are the problem, not so much physical pain or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to do certain things over and over, like checking or cleaning, because of these worries?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I have to go wash my hands a bunch of times or keep checking if the stove is off. It's more just... checking things in my head, if that makes sense. But physically doing things over and over? No, I don't do that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind that you try to ignore or get rid of?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they definitely repeat. I try to stop them. I'll tell myself, Sunny, it's handled, but the thought just keeps looping anyway. And pretty soon I've gone from that one concern right to another one, and I just end up checking all these details in my head over and over again. So I do try to ignore them or push them away, but they just keep running.\n\nDoctor: Are these looping thoughts you're experiencing unwanted or intrusive?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use the word intrusive. I mean, obviously I don't want to be worrying all the time, so I guess they're unwanted in that way. But it's not like weird or random thoughts are just popping into my head out of nowhere. They're just my normal, everyday concerns about work or my schedule... they just kind of get stuck on a loop and I can't seem to turn them off.\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these looping thoughts cause you?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to measure it, honestly. I mean, it's definitely annoying. It's frustrating when I just want to relax and my brain won't shut off. But it's not like I'm falling apart over it or anything like that. It just kind of wears me out, having to deal with it all the time... but I still get through my day fine.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts that you consider unreasonable or excessive that keep repeating in your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say they're unreasonable. Like I said, they're just normal things I need to take care of—like scheduling at the clinic or making sure bills are paid. It's just the amount of time I spend thinking about them that feels excessive, not the thoughts themselves.\n\nDoctor: How much do these looping thoughts and worries affect your ability to perform your daily tasks and enjoy your free time?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m still showing up and getting through the day just fine. But... I guess it does slow me down a bit at work. Like, scheduling takes me a lot longer now because I end up double-checking myself so much. And there have been a few times recently where I’ve had to redo parts of the lobby displays because I just... lost track of what I was arranging. So I'm still getting my tasks done, it just takes more effort than it used to.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations that you used to enjoy but now avoid because of your worries or concerns?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I still do all the same things I used to. I still like my hobbies and hanging out with my family. The worries don't really stop me from doing anything, they're just... there in the background while I'm doing them.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries or looping thoughts affected your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, my family might notice that I'm a little distracted sometimes if my mind is on other things, but it hasn't caused any arguments or real problems. We still get along fine, and I still see my friends like usual. So, no, I wouldn't say it's affected my relationships. It's mostly just something I'm dealing with internally.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily about the original mix-up with the paperwork or have they expanded to include a wide range of everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's definitely expanded. I mean, it started with that original mix-up, but now... I just find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Like work schedules, bills, travel plans... even just little paperwork errors. It's like my brain tells me that one small mix-up is going to somehow turn into a much bigger problem. So, yeah, it's definitely not just that first thing anymore, it's a lot of everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts would stop or significantly decrease if the original cause, such as the mix-up with the paperwork, was fully resolved?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure. I mean... it would definitely be a relief to have that original thing completely sorted out, but since my mind has already started stressing over bills and schedules and all those other things... I don't know if fixing that first problem would actually make the rest of it stop now. I'd like to think it would help, but honestly, I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are primarily driven by a fear of something going wrong in general, or are they more about the consequences of specific events or stressors?\n\nPatient: I guess... I'm not really sure how to separate those two things. I mean, I worry about specific things like the bills or my schedule at work, but the feeling behind it is just this... general sense that something is going to get messed up. It's like I'm waiting for the other shoe to drop, so I don't know if it's the specific event or just the fear of things going wrong overall.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your looping thoughts and worries would be significantly reduced if you didn't have any upcoming events or tasks that you feel responsible for?\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure. I mean... it sounds nice to not have anything on my plate for a while. But knowing how my mind works lately, I'd probably just find something else to stress over. Even if I didn't have any immediate tasks or responsibilities, I think I'd still be looping over things that could happen down the road. So... I don't really know if it would make a significant difference.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries and looping thoughts are more about everyday concerns in general or specifically about coping with the aftermath of the detention and paperwork mix-up?\n\nPatient: Well, I think it's definitely shifted to just everyday concerns now. I mean, the paperwork mix-up was the start, but lately... I find myself worrying about all kinds of ordinary things. Work schedules, paying the bills, travel plans, even just little, normal paperwork errors at my job. \n\nIt's just... it feels like if there's one small mix-up with any of it, it's going to snowball and turn into a much bigger problem. So yeah, it's definitely spread out to just regular, everyday stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your looping thoughts and worries would completely go away if there were no stressors or problems in your life?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know. I mean... it's kind of hard to even picture a life with absolutely zero problems. But if I had to guess... probably not. \n\nLike I was saying earlier, my mind just sort of looks for things to stress over right now. Even if everything was perfectly fine and there were no actual stressors, I feel like I'd still be sitting there waiting for something bad to happen. So, no, I don't think the worrying would just completely disappear.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries would occur even if you didn't have any current problems or stressors in your life?\n\nPatient: I think... well, I feel like I kind of just answered that, but yeah. Even if I didn't have any current problems right now, I'm pretty sure I'd still be worrying. My mind just doesn't seem to know how to turn it off, regardless of what's actually going on."
